# 33 Low-power timer (LPTIM)

## 33.1 Introduction

The LPTIM is a 16-bit timer that benefits from the ultimate developments in power consumption reduction. Thanks to its diversity of clock sources, the LPTIM is able to keep running in all power modes except for Standby mode. Given its capability to run even with no internal clock source, the LPTIM can be used as a “Pulse Counter” which can be useful in some applications. Also, the LPTIM capability to wake up the system from low-power modes, makes it suitable to realize “Timeout functions” with extremely low power consumption.

The LPTIM introduces a flexible clock scheme that provides the needed functionalities and performance, while minimizing the power consumption.

## 33.2 LPTIM main features

- 16 bit upcounter
- 3-bit prescaler with 8 possible dividing factors (1,2,4,8,16,32,64,128)
- Selectable clock
<br>- Internal clock sources: configurable internal clock source (see RCC section)
<br>- External clock source over LPTIM input (working with no embedded oscillator running, used by Pulse Counter application)
- 16 bit ARR autoreload register
- 16 bit compare register
- Continuous/One-shot mode
- Selectable software/hardware input trigger
- Programmable Digital Glitch filter
- Configurable output: Pulse, PWM
- Configurable I/O polarity
- Encoder mode

### 33.4.3 LPTIM reset and clocks

The LPTIM can be clocked using several clock sources. It can be clocked using an internal clock signal which can be any configurable internal clock source selectable through the RCC (see RCC section for more details). Also, the LPTIM can be clocked using an external clock signal injected on its external Input1. When clocked with an external clock source, the LPTIM may run in one of these two possible configurations:

- The first configuration is when the LPTIM is clocked by an external signal but in the same time an internal clock signal is provided to the LPTIM from configurable internal clock source (see RCC section).
- The second configuration is when the LPTIM is solely clocked by an external clock source through its external Input1. This configuration is the one used to realize Timeout function or Pulse counter function when all the embedded oscillators are turned off after entering a low-power mode.

Programming the CKSEL and COUNTMODE bits allows controlling whether the LPTIM will use an external clock source or an internal one.

When configured to use an external clock source, the CKPOL bits are used to select the external clock signal active edge. If both edges are configured to be active ones, an internal clock signal should also be provided (first configuration). In this case, the internal clock signal frequency should be at least four times higher than the external clock signal frequency.

### 33.4.4 Glitch filter

The LPTIM inputs, either external (mapped to GPIOs) or internal (mapped on the chip-level to other embedded peripherals), are protected with digital filters that prevent any glitches and noise perturbations to propagate inside the LPTIM. This is in order to prevent spurious counts or triggers.

Before activating the digital filters, an internal clock source should first be provided to the LPTIM. This is necessary to guarantee the proper operation of the filters.

The digital filters are divided into two groups:

- The first group of digital filters protects the LPTIM external inputs. The digital filters sensitivity is controlled by the CKFLT bits
- The second group of digital filters protects the LPTIM internal trigger inputs. The digital filters sensitivity is controlled by the TRGFLT bits.

**Note:**

The digital filters sensitivity is controlled by groups. It is not possible to configure each digital filter sensitivity separately inside the same group.

The filter sensitivity acts on the number of consecutive equal samples that should be detected on one of the LPTIM inputs to consider a signal level change as a valid transition. [Figure 522](#XREF_24436__Figure_193__Glich_filter_timing_diagram33) shows an example of glitch filter behavior in case of a 2 consecutive samples programmed.

<a id="XREF_24436__Figure_193__Glich_filter_timing_diagram33"></a>

#### Figure 522. Glitch filter timing diagram![](MS32490V1.svg)

**Note:**

In case no internal clock signal is provided, the digital filter must be deactivated by setting the CKFLT and TRGFLT bits to ‘0’. In that case, an external analog filter may be used to protect the LPTIM external inputs against glitches.

### 33.4.5 Prescaler

The LPTIM 16-bit counter is preceded by a configurable power-of-2 prescaler. The prescaler division ratio is controlled by the PRESC[2:0] 3-bit field. The table below lists all the possible division ratios:

 


<div align="center"><table class="TableTitle"><caption class="SoftTableCap"><h4 class="TableCap" id="1236798">Table 330. Prescaler division ratios</h4></caption><col width="269.29px"/><col width="269.29px"/><thead><tr height="28.00px"><th><p class="TableHeadC">programming</p></th><th><p class="TableHeadC">dividing factor</p></th></tr></thead><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="TableBodyC">000</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyC">/1</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="TableBodyC">001</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyC">/2</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="TableBodyC">010</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyC">/4</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="TableBodyC">011</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyC">/8</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="TableBodyC">100</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyC">/16</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="TableBodyC">101</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyC">/32</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="TableBodyC">110</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyC">/64</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="TableBodyC">111</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyC">/128</p></td></tr></table></div>


### 33.4.6 Trigger multiplexer

The LPTIM counter may be started either by software or after the detection of an active edge on one of the 13 trigger inputs.

TRIGEN[1:0] is used to determine the LPTIM trigger source:

- When TRIGEN[1:0] equals ‘00’, The LPTIM counter is started as soon as one of the CNTSTRT or the SNGSTRT bits is set by software. The three remaining possible values for the TRIGEN[1:0] are used to configure the active edge used by the trigger inputs. The LPTIM counter starts as soon as an active edge is detected.
- When TRIGEN[1:0] is different than ‘00’, TRIGSEL[2:0] is used to select which of the 13 trigger inputs is used to start the counter.

The external triggers are considered asynchronous signals for the LPTIM. So after a trigger detection, a two-counter-clock period latency is needed before the timer starts running due to the synchronization.

If a new trigger event occurs when the timer is already started it will be ignored (unless timeout function is enabled).

**Note:**

The timer must be enabled before setting the SNGSTRT/CNTSTRT bits. Any write on these bits when the timer is disabled will be discarded by hardware.

**Note:**

When starting the counter by software (TRIGEN[1:0] = 00), there is a delay of 3 kernel clock cycles between the LPTIM\_CR register update (set one of SNGSTRT or CNTSTRT bits) and the effective start of the counter.

### 33.4.7 Operating mode

The LPTIM features two operating modes:

- The Continuous mode: the timer is free running, the timer is started from a trigger event and never stops until the timer is disabled
- One-shot mode: the timer is started from a trigger event and stops when reaching the ARR value.

#### One-shot mode

To enable the one-shot counting, the SNGSTRT bit must be set.

A new trigger event will re-start the timer. Any trigger event occurring after the counter starts and before the counter reaches ARR will be discarded.

In case an external trigger is selected, each external trigger event arriving after the SNGSTRT bit is set, and after the counter register has stopped (contains zero value), will start the counter for a new one-shot counting cycle as shown in [Figure 523](#XREF_93033__Figure_315_33).

<a id="XREF_93033__Figure_315_33"></a>

#### Figure 523. LPTIM output waveform, single counting mode configuration![](MSv39230V2.svg)

Set-once mode activated:

It should be noted that when the WAVE bit-field in the LPTIM\_CFGR register is set, the Set-once mode is activated. In this case, the counter is only started once following the first trigger, and any subsequent trigger event is discarded as shown in [Figure 524](#XREF_96105__Figure_316_33).

<a id="XREF_96105__Figure_316_33"></a>

#### Figure 524. LPTIM output waveform, Single counting mode configuration    and Set-once mode activated (WAVE bit is set)![](MSv39231V2.svg)

In case of software start (TRIGEN[1:0] = ‘00’), the SNGSTRT setting will start the counter for one-shot counting.

#### Continous mode

To enable the continuous counting, the CNTSTRT bit must be set.

In case an external trigger is selected, an external trigger event arriving after CNTSTRT is set will start the counter for continuous counting. Any subsequent external trigger event will be discarded as shown in [Figure 525](#XREF_68696__Figure_317_33).

In case of software start (TRIGEN[1:0] = ‘00’), setting CNTSTRT will start the counter for continuous counting.

<a id="XREF_68696__Figure_317_33"></a>

#### Figure 525. LPTIM output waveform, Continuous counting mode configuration![](MSv39229V2.svg)

SNGSTRT and CNTSTRT bits can only be set when the timer is enabled (The ENABLE bit is set to ‘1’). It is possible to change “on the fly” from One-shot mode to Continuous mode.

If the Continuous mode was previously selected, setting SNGSTRT will switch the LPTIM to the One-shot mode. The counter (if active) will stop as soon as it reaches ARR.

If the One-shot mode was previously selected, setting CNTSTRT will switch the LPTIM to the Continuous mode. The counter (if active) will restart as soon as it reaches ARR.

### 33.4.8 Timeout function

The detection of an active edge on one selected trigger input can be used to reset the LPTIM counter. This feature is controlled through the TIMOUT bit.

The first trigger event will start the timer, any successive trigger event will reset the counter and the timer will restart.

A low-power timeout function can be realized. The timeout value corresponds to the compare value; if no trigger occurs within the expected time frame, the MCU is waked-up by the compare match event.

### 33.4.9 Waveform generation

Two 16-bit registers, the LPTIM\_ARR (autoreload register) and LPTIM\_CMP (compare register), are used to generate several different waveforms on LPTIM output

The timer can generate the following waveforms:

- The PWM mode: the LPTIM output is set as soon as the counter value in LPTIM\_CNT exceeds the compare value in LPTIM\_CMP. The LPTIM output is reset as soon as a match occurs between the LPTIM\_ARR and the LPTIM\_CNT registers.
- The One-pulse mode: the output waveform is similar to the one of the PWM mode for the first pulse, then the output is permanently reset
- The Set-once mode: the output waveform is similar to the One-pulse mode except that the output is kept to the last signal level (depends on the output configured polarity).

The above described modes require that the LPTIM\_ARR register value be strictly greater than the LPTIM\_CMP register value.

The LPTIM output waveform can be configured through the WAVE bit as follow:

- Resetting the WAVE bit to ‘0’ forces the LPTIM to generate either a PWM waveform or a One pulse waveform depending on which bit is set: CNTSTRT or SNGSTRT.
- Setting the WAVE bit to ‘1’ forces the LPTIM to generate a Set-once mode waveform.

The WAVPOL bit controls the LPTIM output polarity. The change takes effect immediately, so the output default value will change immediately after the polarity is re-configured, even before the timer is enabled.

Signals with frequencies up to the LPTIM clock frequency divided by 2 can be generated. [Figure 526](#XREF_67352__Figure_194__Waveform_generation33) below shows the three possible waveforms that can be generated on the LPTIM output. Also, it shows the effect of the polarity change using the WAVPOL bit.

<a id="XREF_67352__Figure_194__Waveform_generation33"></a>

#### Figure 526. Waveform generation![](MS32467V2.svg)

### 33.4.10 Register update

The LPTIM\_ARR register and LPTIM\_CMP register are updated immediately after the APB bus write operation, or at the end of the current period if the timer is already started.

The PRELOAD bit controls how the LPTIM\_ARR and the LPTIM\_CMP registers are updated:

- When the PRELOAD bit is reset to ‘0’, the LPTIM\_ARR and the LPTIM\_CMP registers are immediately updated after any write access.
- When the PRELOAD bit is set to ‘1’, the LPTIM\_ARR and the LPTIM\_CMP registers are updated at the end of the current period, if the timer has been already started.

The LPTIM APB interface and the LPTIM kernel logic use different clocks, so there is some latency between the APB write and the moment when these values are available to the counter comparator. Within this latency period, any additional write into these registers must be avoided.

The ARROK flag and the CMPOK flag in the LPTIM\_ISR register indicate when the write operation is completed to respectively the LPTIM\_ARR register and the LPTIM\_CMP register.

After a write to the LPTIM\_ARR register or the LPTIM\_CMP register, a new write operation to the same register can only be performed when the previous write operation is completed. Any successive write before respectively the ARROK flag or the CMPOK flag be set, will lead to unpredictable results.

### 33.4.11 Counter mode

The LPTIM counter can be used to count external events on the LPTIM Input1 or it can be used to count internal clock cycles. The CKSEL and COUNTMODE bits control which source will be used for updating the counter.

In case the LPTIM is configured to count external events on Input1, the counter can be updated following a rising edge, falling edge or both edges depending on the value written to the CKPOL[1:0] bits.

The count modes below can be selected, depending on CKSEL and COUNTMODE values:

- CKSEL = 0: the LPTIM is clocked by an internal clock source
<br>- COUNTMODE = 0

The LPTIM is configured to be clocked by an internal clock source and the LPTIM counter is configured to be updated following each internal clock pulse.

<br>- COUNTMODE = 1

The LPTIM external Input1 is sampled with the internal clock provided to the LPTIM.

Consequently, in order not to miss any event, the frequency of the changes on the external Input1 signal should never exceed the frequency of the internal clock provided to the LPTIM. Also, the internal clock provided to the LPTIM must not be prescaled (PRESC[2:0] = 000).

- CKSEL = 1: the LPTIM is clocked by an external clock source

COUNTMODE value is don’t care.

In this configuration, the LPTIM has no need for an internal clock source (except if the glitch filters are enabled). The signal injected on the LPTIM external Input1 is used as system clock for the LPTIM. This configuration is suitable for operation modes where no embedded oscillator is enabled.

For this configuration, the LPTIM counter can be updated either on rising edges or falling edges of the input1 clock signal but not on both rising and falling edges.

Since the signal injected on the LPTIM external Input1 is also used to clock the LPTIM kernel logic, there is some initial latency (after the LPTIM is enabled) before the counter is incremented. More precisely, the first five active edges on the LPTIM external Input1 (after LPTIM is enable) are lost.

### 33.4.12 Timer enable

The ENABLE bit located in the LPTIM\_CR register is used to enable/disable the LPTIM kernel logic. After setting the ENABLE bit, a delay of two counter clock is needed before the LPTIM is actually enabled.

The LPTIM\_CFGR and LPTIM\_IER registers must be modified only when the LPTIM is disabled.

### 33.4.13 Timer counter reset

In order to reset the content of LPTIM\_CNT register to zero, two reset mechanisms are implemented:

- The synchronous reset mechanism: the synchronous reset is controlled by the COUNTRST bit in the LPTIM\_CR register. After setting the COUNTRST bit-field to '1', the reset signal is propagated in the LPTIM kernel clock domain. So it is important to note that a few clock pulses of the LPTIM kernel logic will elapse before the reset is taken into account. This will make the LPTIM counter count few extra pluses between the time when the reset is trigger and it become effective. Since the COUNTRST bit is located in the APB clock domain and the LPTIM counter is located in the LPTIM kernel clock domain, a delay of 3 clock cycles of the kernel clock is needed to synchronize the reset signal issued by the APB clock domain when writing '1' to the COUNTRST bit.
- The asynchronous reset mechanism: the asynchronous reset is controlled by the RSTARE bit located in the LPTIM\_CR register. When this bit is set to '1', any read access to the LPTIM\_CNT register will reset its content to zero. Asynchronous reset should be triggered within a timeframe in which no LPTIM core clock is provided. For example when LPTIM Input1 is used as external clock source, the asynchronous reset should be applied only when there is enough insurance that no toggle will occur on the LPTIM Input1.  
  It should be noted that to read reliably the content of the LPTIM\_CNT register two successive read accesses must be performed and compared. A read access can be considered reliable when the value of the two read accesses is equal. Unfortunately when asynchronous reset is enabled there is no possibility to read twice the LPTIM\_CNT register.

Warning:There is no mechanism inside the LPTIM that prevents the two reset mechanisms from being used simultaneously. So developer should make sure that these two mechanisms are used exclusively.

### 33.4.14 Encoder mode

This mode allows handling signals from quadrature encoders used to detect angular position of rotary elements. Encoder interface mode acts simply as an external clock with direction selection. This means that the counter just counts continuously between 0 and the auto-reload value programmed into the LPTIM\_ARR register (0 up to ARR or ARR down to 0 depending on the direction). Therefore LPTIM\_ARR must be configured before starting the counter. From the two external input signals, Input1 and Input2, a clock signal is generated to clock the LPTIM counter. The phase between those two signals determines the counting direction.

The Encoder mode is only available when the LPTIM is clocked by an internal clock source. The signals frequency on both Input1 and Input2 inputs must not exceed the LPTIM internal clock frequency divided by 4. This is mandatory in order to guarantee a proper operation of the LPTIM.

Direction change is signalized by the two Down and Up flags in the LPTIM\_ISR register. Also, an interrupt can be generated for both direction change events if enabled through the DOWNIE bit.

To activate the Encoder mode the ENC bit has to be set to ‘1’. The LPTIM must first be configured in Continuous mode.

When Encoder mode is active, the LPTIM counter is modified automatically following the speed and the direction of the incremental encoder. Therefore, its content always represents the encoder’s position. The count direction, signaled by the Up and Down flags, correspond to the rotation direction of the encoder rotor.

According to the edge sensitivity configured using the CKPOL[1:0] bits, different counting scenarios are possible. The following table summarizes the possible combinations, assuming that Input1 and Input2 do not switch at the same time.

### 33.4.15 Debug mode

When the microcontroller enters debug mode (core halted), the LPTIM counter either continues to work normally or stops, depending on the DBG\_LPTIM\_STOP configuration bit in the DBG module.

## 33.6 LPTIM interrupts

The following events generate an interrupt/wake-up event, if they are enabled through the LPTIM\_IER register:

- Compare match
- Auto-reload match (whatever the direction if encoder mode)
- External trigger event
- Autoreload register write completed
- Compare register write completed
- Direction change (encoder mode), programmable (up / down / both).

**Note:**

If any bit in the LPTIM\_IER register (Interrupt Enable Register) is set after that its corresponding flag in the LPTIM\_ISR register (Status Register) is set, the interrupt is not asserted.

 


<div align="center"><table class="TableTitle"><caption class="Body"><h4 class="TableCap" id="17753583">Table 333. <a id="(continued)33" name="XREF_73830__Table_199__Interrupt_events_(continued)33">Interrupt events</a></h4></caption><col width="132.09px"/><col width="406.49px"/><thead><tr height="28.00px"><th><p class="TableHeadC"><a id="17753587" name="294_TableHeadC_17753587"></a>Interrupt event</p></th><th><p class="TableHeadC">Description</p></th></tr></thead><tr height="37.33px"><td><p class="TableBodyL">Compare match</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Interrupt flag is raised when the content of the Counter register (LPTIM_CNT) matches the content of the compare register (LPTIM_CMP).</p></td></tr><tr height="52.00px"><td><p class="TableBodyL">Auto-reload match</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Interrupt flag is raised when the content of the Counter register (LPTIM_CNT) matches the content of the Auto-reload register (LPTIM_ARR).</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="TableBodyL">External trigger event</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Interrupt flag is raised when an external trigger event is detected</p></td></tr><tr height="37.33px"><td><p class="TableBodyL">Auto-reload register update OK</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Interrupt flag is raised when the write operation to the LPTIM_ARR register is complete.</p></td></tr><tr height="37.33px"><td><p class="TableBodyL">Compare register update OK</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Interrupt flag is raised when the write operation to the LPTIM_CMP register is complete.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_lptimer1v1_encoder_ck" height="72.00px"><td data-conditional="_lptimer1v1_encoder_ck"><p class="TableBodyL">Direction change</p></td><td data-conditional="_lptimer1v1_encoder_ck"><p class="TableBodyL">Used in Encoder mode. Two interrupt flags are embedded to signal direction change:</p><li class="TableBullet" id="17753614">–<span style="padding-right:3pt ;">​</span>UP flag signals up-counting direction change</li><li class="TableBullet" id="17753615">–<span style="padding-right:3pt ;">​</span>DOWN flag signals down-counting direction change.</li></td></tr></table></div>

<div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="74.42px"/><col width="539.75px"/><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 31:7</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_nolptimer1v1_encoder_ck" height="22.67px" style="display: none"><td data-conditional="_nolptimer1v1_encoder_ck" style="display: none"></td><td data-conditional="_nolptimer1v1_encoder_ck" style="display: none"></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_lptimer1v1_encoder_ck" height="101.33px"><td data-conditional="_lptimer1v1_encoder_ck"><p class="RegTBodyR"><span class="ConditionalText_Encoder_CK">Bit 6</span></p></td><td data-conditional="_lptimer1v1_encoder_ck"><p class="TableBodyL"><span class="ConditionalText_Encoder_CK" style="font-weight:Bold;">DOWN</span><span class="ConditionalText_Encoder_CK">: Counter direction change up to down</span></p><p class="TableIndent"><span class="ConditionalText_Encoder_CK">In Encoder mode, DOWN bit is set by hardware to inform application that the counter direction has changed from up to down. DOWN flag can be cleared by writing 1 to the DOWNCF bit in the LPTIM_ICR register.</span></p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>If the LPTIM does not support encoder mode feature, this bit is reserved. Please refer to <a href="#XREF_10242__26_3_LPTIM_implementation33" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;font-kerning:normal;font-stretch:normal;color:#0000ff;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Section 33.3: LPTIM implementation</a>.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_lptimer1v1_encoder_ck" height="101.33px"><td data-conditional="_lptimer1v1_encoder_ck"><p class="RegTBodyR"><span class="ConditionalText_Encoder_CK">Bit 5</span></p></td><td data-conditional="_lptimer1v1_encoder_ck"><p class="TableBodyL"><span class="ConditionalText_Encoder_CK" style="font-weight:Bold;">UP</span><span class="ConditionalText_Encoder_CK">: Counter direction change down to up</span></p><p class="TableIndent"><span class="ConditionalText_Encoder_CK">In Encoder mode, UP bit is set by hardware to inform application that the counter direction has changed from down to up. UP flag can be cleared by writing 1 to the UPCF bit in the LPTIM_ICR register.</span></p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>If the LPTIM does not support encoder mode feature, this bit is reserved. Please refer to <a href="#XREF_10242__26_3_LPTIM_implementation33" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;font-kerning:normal;font-stretch:normal;color:#0000ff;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Section 33.3: LPTIM implementation</a>.</p></td></tr><tr height="69.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 4</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">ARROK</span>: Autoreload register update OK</p><p class="TableIndent">ARROK is set by hardware to inform application that the APB bus write operation to the LPTIM_ARR register has been successfully completed. ARROK flag can be cleared by writing 1 to the ARROKCF bit in the LPTIM_ICR register.</p></td></tr><tr height="69.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 3</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">CMPOK</span>: Compare register update OK</p><p class="TableIndent">CMPOK is set by hardware to inform application that the APB bus write operation to the LPTIM_CMP register has been successfully completed. CMPOK flag can be cleared by writing 1 to the CMPOKCF bit in the LPTIM_ICR register.</p></td></tr><tr height="69.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 2</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">EXTTRIG</span>: External trigger edge event</p><p class="TableIndent">EXTTRIG is set by hardware to inform application that a valid edge on the selected external trigger input has occurred. If the trigger is ignored because the timer has already started, then this flag is not set. EXTTRIG flag can be cleared by writing 1 to the EXTTRIGCF bit in the LPTIM_ICR register.</p></td></tr><tr height="69.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 1</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">ARRM</span>: Autoreload match</p><p class="TableIndent">ARRM is set by hardware to inform application that LPTIM_CNT register’s value reached the LPTIM_ARR register’s value. ARRM flag can be cleared by writing 1 to the ARRMCF bit in the LPTIM_ICR register.</p></td></tr><tr height="69.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">CMPM</span>: Compare match</p><p class="TableIndent">The CMPM bit is set by hardware to inform application that LPTIM_CNT register value reached the LPTIM_CMP register’s value. CMPM flag can be cleared by writing 1 to the CMPMCF bit in the LPTIM_ICR register.</p></td></tr></table></div>


### 33.7.2 LPTIM interrupt clear register (LPTIM\_ICR)

Address offset: 0x004

Reset value: 0x0000 0000

<div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="74.42px"/><col width="539.75px"/><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 31:7</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_nolptimer1v1_encoder_ck" height="22.67px" style="display: none"><td data-conditional="_nolptimer1v1_encoder_ck" style="display: none"></td><td data-conditional="_nolptimer1v1_encoder_ck" style="display: none"></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_lptimer1v1_encoder_ck" height="72.00px"><td data-conditional="_lptimer1v1_encoder_ck"><p class="RegTBodyR"><span class="ConditionalText_Encoder_CK">Bit 6</span></p></td><td data-conditional="_lptimer1v1_encoder_ck"><p class="TableBodyL"><span class="ConditionalText_Encoder_CK" style="font-weight:Bold;">DOWNCF</span><span class="ConditionalText_Encoder_CK">: Direction change to down clear flag</span></p><p class="TableIndent"><span class="ConditionalText_Encoder_CK">Writing 1 to this bit clear the DOWN flag in the LPTIM_ISR register.</span></p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>If the LPTIM does not support encoder mode feature, this bit is reserved. Please refer to <a href="#XREF_10242__26_3_LPTIM_implementation33" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;font-kerning:normal;font-stretch:normal;color:#0000ff;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Section 33.3: LPTIM implementation</a>.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_lptimer1v1_encoder_ck" height="72.00px"><td data-conditional="_lptimer1v1_encoder_ck"><p class="RegTBodyR"><span class="ConditionalText_Encoder_CK">Bit 5</span></p></td><td data-conditional="_lptimer1v1_encoder_ck"><p class="TableBodyL"><span class="ConditionalText_Encoder_CK" style="font-weight:Bold;">UPCF</span><span class="ConditionalText_Encoder_CK">: Direction change to UP clear flag</span></p><p class="TableIndent"><span class="ConditionalText_Encoder_CK">Writing 1 to this bit clear the UP flag in the LPTIM_ISR register.</span></p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>If the LPTIM does not support encoder mode feature, this bit is reserved. Please refer to <a href="#XREF_10242__26_3_LPTIM_implementation33" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;font-kerning:normal;font-stretch:normal;color:#0000ff;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Section 33.3: LPTIM implementation</a>.</p></td></tr><tr height="40.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 4</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">ARROKCF</span>: Autoreload register update OK clear flag</p><p class="TableIndent">Writing 1 to this bit clears the ARROK flag in the LPTIM_ISR register</p></td></tr><tr height="40.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 3</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">CMPOKCF</span>: Compare register update OK clear flag</p><p class="TableIndent">Writing 1 to this bit clears the CMPOK flag in the LPTIM_ISR register</p></td></tr><tr height="40.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 2</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">EXTTRIGCF</span>: External trigger valid edge clear flag</p><p class="TableIndent">Writing 1 to this bit clears the EXTTRIG flag in the LPTIM_ISR register</p></td></tr><tr height="40.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 1</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">ARRMCF</span>: Autoreload match clear flag</p><p class="TableIndent">Writing 1 to this bit clears the ARRM flag in the LPTIM_ISR register</p></td></tr><tr height="40.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">CMPMCF</span>: Compare match clear flag</p><p class="TableIndent">Writing 1 to this bit clears the CMPM flag in the LPTIM_ISR register</p></td></tr></table></div>


### 33.7.3 LPTIM interrupt enable register (LPTIM\_IER)

Address offset: 0x008

Reset value: 0x0000 0000

Please refer to <a href="#XREF_10242__26_3_LPTIM_implementation33" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;font-kerning:normal;font-stretch:normal;color:#0000ff;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Section 33.3: LPTIM implementation</a>.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_lptimer1v1_encoder_ck" height="88.00px"><td data-conditional="_lptimer1v1_encoder_ck"><p class="RegTBodyR"><span class="ConditionalText_Encoder_CK">Bit 5</span></p></td><td data-conditional="_lptimer1v1_encoder_ck"><p class="TableBodyL"><span class="ConditionalText_Encoder_CK" style="font-weight:Bold;">UPIE</span><span class="ConditionalText_Encoder_CK">: Direction change to UP Interrupt Enable</span></p><p class="TableIndent" style="margin-left : 25.51pt;"><span class="ConditionalText_Encoder_CK">0:   UP interrupt disabled</span></p><p class="TableIndent" style="margin-left : 25.51pt;"><span class="ConditionalText_Encoder_CK">1:   UP interrupt enabled</span></p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>If the LPTIM does not support encoder mode feature, this bit is reserved. Please refer to <a href="#XREF_10242__26_3_LPTIM_implementation33" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;font-kerning:normal;font-stretch:normal;color:#0000ff;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Section 33.3: LPTIM implementation</a>.</p></td></tr><tr height="56.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 4</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">ARROKIE</span>: Autoreload register update OK Interrupt Enable</p><p class="TableIndent" style="margin-left : 25.51pt;">0:   ARROK interrupt disabled</p><p class="TableIndent" style="margin-left : 25.51pt;">1:   ARROK interrupt enabled</p></td></tr><tr height="56.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 3</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">CMPOKIE</span>: Compare register update OK Interrupt Enable</p><p class="TableIndent" style="margin-left : 25.51pt;">0:   CMPOK interrupt disabled</p><p class="TableIndent" style="margin-left : 25.51pt;">1:   CMPOK interrupt enabled</p></td></tr><tr height="56.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 2</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">EXTTRIGIE</span>: External trigger valid edge Interrupt Enable</p><p class="TableIndent" style="margin-left : 25.51pt;">0:   EXTTRIG interrupt disabled</p><p class="TableIndent" style="margin-left : 25.51pt;">1:   EXTTRIG interrupt enabled</p></td></tr><tr height="56.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 1</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">ARRMIE</span>: Autoreload match Interrupt Enable</p><p class="TableIndent" style="margin-left : 25.51pt;">0:   ARRM interrupt disabled</p><p class="TableIndent" style="margin-left : 25.51pt;">1:   ARRM interrupt enabled</p></td></tr><tr height="56.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">CMPMIE</span>: Compare match Interrupt Enable</p><p class="TableIndent" style="margin-left : 25.51pt;">0:   CMPM interrupt disabled</p><p class="TableIndent" style="margin-left : 25.51pt;">1:   CMPM interrupt enabled</p></td></tr></table></div>


Caution:The LPTIM\_IER register must only be modified when the LPTIM is disabled (ENABLE bit reset to ‘0’)

### 33.7.4 LPTIM configuration register (LPTIM\_CFGR)

Address offset: 0x00C

Reset value: 0x0000 0000

<div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="76.05px"/><col width="538.12px"/><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 31:30</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_lptimer1v1_8trig" height="22.67px" style="display: none"><td data-conditional="_lptimer1v1_8trig" style="display: none"></td><td data-conditional="_lptimer1v1_8trig" style="display: none"></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 28:<span data-conditional="_lptimer1v1_encoder_ck">25</span></p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_lptimer1v1_encoder_ck" height="104.00px"><td data-conditional="_lptimer1v1_encoder_ck"><p class="RegTBodyR"><span class="ConditionalText_Encoder_CK">Bit 24</span></p></td><td data-conditional="_lptimer1v1_encoder_ck"><p class="TableBodyL"><span class="ConditionalText_Encoder_CK" style="font-weight:Bold;">ENC</span><span class="ConditionalText_Encoder_CK">: Encoder mode enable</span></p><p class="TableIndent"><span class="ConditionalText_Encoder_CK">The ENC bit controls the Encoder mode</span></p><p class="TableIndent" style="margin-left : 25.51pt;"><span class="ConditionalText_Encoder_CK">0:   Encoder mode disabled</span></p><p class="TableIndent" style="margin-left : 25.51pt;"><span class="ConditionalText_Encoder_CK">1:   Encoder mode enabled</span></p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>If the LPTIM does not support encoder mode feature, this bit is reserved. Please refer to <a href="#XREF_10242__26_3_LPTIM_implementation33" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;font-kerning:normal;font-stretch:normal;color:#0000ff;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Section 33.3: LPTIM implementation</a>.</p></td></tr><tr height="72.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 23</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">COUNTMODE</span>: counter mode enabled</p><p class="TableIndent">The COUNTMODE bit selects which clock source is used by the LPTIM to clock the counter:</p><p class="TableIndent" style="margin-left : 25.51pt;">0:   the counter is incremented following each internal clock pulse</p><p class="TableIndent" style="margin-left : 25.51pt;">1:   the counter is incremented following each valid clock pulse on the LPTIM external Input1</p></td></tr><tr height="72.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 22</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">PRELOAD</span>: Registers update mode</p><p class="TableIndent">The PRELOAD bit controls the LPTIM_ARR and the LPTIM_CMP registers update modality</p><p class="TableIndent" style="margin-left : 25.51pt;">0:   Registers are updated after each APB bus write access</p><p class="TableIndent" style="margin-left : 25.51pt;">1:   Registers are updated at the end of the current LPTIM period</p></td></tr><tr height="117.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 21</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">WAVPOL</span>: Waveform shape polarity</p><p class="TableIndent">The WAVEPOL bit controls the output polarity</p><p class="TableIndent" style="margin-left : 25.51pt;">0:   The LPTIM output reflects the compare results between LPTIM_CNT and LPTIM_CMP registers</p><p class="TableIndent" style="margin-left : 25.51pt;">1:   The LPTIM output reflects the inverse of the compare results between LPTIM_CNT and LPTIM_CMP registers</p></td></tr><tr height="72.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 20</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">WAVE</span>: Waveform shape</p><p class="TableIndent">The WAVE bit controls the output shape</p><p class="TableIndent" style="margin-left : 25.51pt;">0:   Deactivate Set-once mode</p><p class="TableIndent" style="margin-left : 25.51pt;">1:   Activate the Set-once mode</p></td></tr><tr height="72.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 19</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">TIMOUT</span>: Timeout enable</p><p class="TableIndent">The TIMOUT bit controls the Timeout feature</p><p class="TableIndent" style="margin-left : 25.51pt;">0:   A trigger event arriving when the timer is already started will be ignored</p><p class="TableIndent" style="margin-left : 25.51pt;">1:   A trigger event arriving when the timer is already started will reset and restart the counter</p></td></tr><tr height="118.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 18:17</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">TRIGEN[1:0]</span>: Trigger enable and polarity</p><p class="TableIndent">The TRIGEN bits controls whether the LPTIM counter is started by an external trigger or not. If the external trigger option is selected, three configurations are possible for the trigger active edge:</p><p class="TableIndent" style="margin-left : 25.51pt;">00:   software trigger (counting start is initiated by software)</p><p class="TableIndent" style="margin-left : 25.51pt;">01:   rising edge is the active edge</p><p class="TableIndent" style="margin-left : 25.51pt;">10:   falling edge is the active edge</p><p class="TableIndent" style="margin-left : 25.51pt;">11:   both edges are active edges</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 16</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_lptimer1v1_16trig" height="294.67px"><td data-conditional="_lptimer1v1_16trig"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 29, 15,14,13</p></td><td data-conditional="_lptimer1v1_16trig"><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">TRIGSEL[3:0]</span>: Trigger selector</p><p class="TableIndent">The TRIGSEL bits select the trigger source that will serve as a trigger event for the LPTIM among the below 13 available sources:</p><p class="TableIndent">0000: lptim_ext_trig0</p><p class="TableIndent">0001: lptim_ext_trig1</p><p class="TableIndent">0010: lptim_ext_trig2</p><p class="TableIndent">0011: lptim_ext_trig3</p><p class="TableIndent">0100: lptim_ext_trig4</p><p class="TableIndent">0101: lptim_ext_trig5</p><p class="TableIndent">0110: lptim_ext_trig6</p><p class="TableIndent">0111: lptim_ext_trig7</p><p class="TableIndent">1000: lptim_ext_trig8</p><p class="TableIndent">1001: lptim_ext_trig9</p><p class="TableIndent">1010: lptim_ext_trig10</p><p class="TableIndent">1011: lptim_ext_trig11</p><p class="TableIndent">1100: lptim_ext_trig12</p><p class="TableIndent">Others: Reserved</p><p class="TableIndent">See <a href="#XREF_21882__34_4_LPTIM_input_and_trigger_mapping33" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;font-kerning:normal;font-stretch:normal;color:#0000ff;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Section 33.4.2: LPTIM input and trigger mapping</a> for details.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_lptimer1v1_8trig" height="198.67px" style="display: none"><td data-conditional="_lptimer1v1_8trig" style="display: none"></td><td data-conditional="_lptimer1v1_8trig" style="display: none"></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 12</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="182.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 11:9</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">PRESC</span><span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:Bold;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">[2:0]:</span> Clock prescaler</p><p class="TableIndent">The PRESC bits configure the prescaler division factor. It can be one among the following division factors:</p><p class="TableIndent" style="margin-left : 34.02pt;">000:   /1</p><p class="TableIndent" style="margin-left : 34.02pt;">001:   /2</p><p class="TableIndent" style="margin-left : 34.02pt;">010:   /4</p><p class="TableIndent" style="margin-left : 34.02pt;">011:   /8</p><p class="TableIndent" style="margin-left : 34.02pt;">100:   /16</p><p class="TableIndent" style="margin-left : 34.02pt;">101:   /32</p><p class="TableIndent" style="margin-left : 34.02pt;">110:   /64</p><p class="TableIndent" style="margin-left : 34.02pt;">111:   /128</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 8</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="177.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 7:6</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">TRGFLT</span><span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:Bold;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">[1:0]:</span> Configurable digital filter for trigger</p><p class="TableIndent">The TRGFLT value sets the number of consecutive equal samples that should be detected when a level change occurs on an internal trigger before it is considered as a valid level transition. An internal clock source must be present to use this feature</p><p class="TableIndent" style="margin-left : 25.51pt;">00:   any trigger active level change is considered as a valid trigger</p><p class="TableIndent" style="margin-left : 25.51pt;">01:   trigger active level change must be stable for at least 2 clock periods before it is considered as valid trigger.</p><p class="TableIndent" style="margin-left : 25.51pt;">10:   trigger active level change must be stable for at least 4 clock periods before it is considered as valid trigger.</p><p class="TableIndent" style="margin-left : 25.51pt;">11:   trigger active level change must be stable for at least 8 clock periods before it is considered as valid trigger.</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 5</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="177.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 4:3</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">CKFLT</span><span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:Bold;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">[1:0]:</span> Configurable digital filter for external clock</p><p class="TableIndent">The CKFLT value sets the number of consecutive equal samples that should be detected when a level change occurs on an external clock signal before it is considered as a valid level transition. An internal clock source must be present to use this feature</p><p class="TableIndent" style="margin-left : 25.51pt;">00:   any external clock signal level change is considered as a valid transition</p><p class="TableIndent" style="margin-left : 25.51pt;">01:   external clock signal level change must be stable for at least 2 clock periods before it is considered as valid transition.</p><p class="TableIndent" style="margin-left : 25.51pt;">10:   external clock signal level change must be stable for at least 4 clock periods before it is considered as valid transition.</p><p class="TableIndent" style="margin-left : 25.51pt;">11:   external clock signal level change must be stable for at least 8 clock periods before it is considered as valid transition.</p></td></tr><tr height="226.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 2:1</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">CKPOL</span><span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:Bold;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">[1:0]:</span> Clock polarity</p><p class="TableBodyL"><span class="ConditionalText_Encoder_CK" data-conditional="_lptimer1v1_encoder_ck">If LPTIM is clocked by an external clock source:</span></p><p class="TableIndent">When the LPTIM is clocked by an external clock source, CKPOL bits is used to configure the active edge or edges used by the counter:</p><p class="TableIndent" style="margin-left : 21.26pt;">00:   the rising edge is the active edge used for counting.<br/>If the LPTIM is configured in Encoder mode (ENC bit is set), the encoder sub-mode 1 is active.</p><p class="TableIndent" style="margin-left : 21.26pt;">01:   the falling edge is the active edge used for counting<br/>If the LPTIM is configured in Encoder mode (ENC bit is set), the encoder sub-mode 2 is active.</p><p class="TableIndent" style="margin-left : 21.26pt;">10:   both edges are active edges. When both external clock signal edges are considered active ones, the LPTIM must also be clocked by an internal clock source with a frequency equal to at least four times the external clock frequency.<br/>If the LPTIM is configured in Encoder mode (ENC bit is set), the encoder sub-mode 3 is active.</p><p class="TableIndent" style="margin-left : 21.26pt;">11:   not allowed</p><p class="TableBodyL"><span class="ConditionalText_Encoder_CK" data-conditional="_lptimer1v1_encoder_ck">Refer to <a href="#XREF_84344__20_3_11_Encoder_mode33" style="font-style:Italic;color:#0000ff;">Section 33.4.14: Encoder mode</a></span><span class="ConditionalText_Encoder_CK" data-conditional="_lptimer1v1_encoder_ck"> for more details about Encoder mode sub-modes.</span></p></td></tr><tr height="72.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">CKSEL:</span> Clock selector</p><p class="TableIndent">The CKSEL bit selects which clock source the LPTIM will use:</p><p class="TableIndent" style="margin-left : 25.51pt;">0:   LPTIM is clocked by internal clock source (APB clock or any of the embedded oscillators)</p><p class="TableIndent" style="margin-left : 25.51pt;">1:   LPTIM is clocked by an external clock source through the LPTIM external Input1</p></td></tr></table></div>


Caution:The LPTIM\_CFGR register must only be modified when the LPTIM is disabled (ENABLE bit reset to ‘0’).

### 33.7.5 LPTIM control register (LPTIM\_CR)

Address offset: 0x010

Reset value: 0x0000 0000

<div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="76.05px"/><col width="538.12px"/><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 31:<span data-conditional="_lptimer1v1_v1_2,_lptimer1v1_v1_3,_lptimer1v1_v1_4">5</span></p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_lptimer1v1_v1_2,_lptimer1v1_v1_3,_lptimer1v1_v1_4" height="88.00px"><td data-conditional="_lptimer1v1_v1_2,_lptimer1v1_v1_3,_lptimer1v1_v1_4"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 4</p></td><td data-conditional="_lptimer1v1_v1_2,_lptimer1v1_v1_3,_lptimer1v1_v1_4"><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">RSTARE:</span> Reset after read enable</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and cleared by software. When RSTARE is set to '1', any read access to LPTIM_CNT register will asynchronously reset LPTIM_CNT register content.</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit can be set only when the LPTIM is enabled.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_lptimer1v1_v1_2,_lptimer1v1_v1_3,_lptimer1v1_v1_4" height="168.00px"><td data-conditional="_lptimer1v1_v1_2,_lptimer1v1_v1_3,_lptimer1v1_v1_4"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 3</p></td><td data-conditional="_lptimer1v1_v1_2,_lptimer1v1_v1_3,_lptimer1v1_v1_4"><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">COUNTRST:</span> Counter reset</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set by software and cleared by hardware. When set to '1' this bit will trigger a synchronous reset of the LPTIM_CNT counter register. Due to the synchronous nature of this reset, it only takes place after a synchronization delay of 3 LPTimer core clock cycles (LPTimer core clock may be different from APB clock).</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit can be set only when the LPTIM is enabled. It is automatically reset by hardware.</p><p class="RegTCaution"><span class="Bold">Caution:<span style="padding-right:8pt ;">​</span></span>COUNTRST must never be set to '1' by software before it is already cleared to '0' by hardware. Software should consequently check that COUNTRST bit is already cleared to '0' before attempting to set it to '1'.</p></td></tr><tr height="148.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 2</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">CNTSTRT:</span> Timer start in Continuous mode</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set by software and cleared by hardware.</p><p class="TableIndent">In case of software start (TRIGEN[1:0] = ‘00’), setting this bit starts the LPTIM in Continuous mode.</p><p class="TableIndent">If the software start is disabled (TRIGEN[1:0] different than ‘00’), setting this bit starts the timer in Continuous mode as soon as an external trigger is detected.</p><p class="TableIndent">If this bit is set when a single pulse mode counting is ongoing, then the timer will not stop at the next match between the LPTIM_ARR and LPTIM_CNT registers and the LPTIM counter keeps counting in Continuous mode.</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit can be set only when the LPTIM is enabled. It will be automatically reset by hardware.</p></td></tr><tr height="133.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 1</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">SNGSTRT:</span> LPTIM start in Single mode</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set by software and cleared by hardware.</p><p class="TableIndent">In case of software start (TRIGEN[1:0] = ‘00’), setting this bit starts the LPTIM in single pulse mode.</p><p class="TableIndent">If the software start is disabled (TRIGEN[1:0] different than ‘00’), setting this bit starts the LPTIM in single pulse mode as soon as an external trigger is detected.</p><p class="TableIndent">If this bit is set when the LPTIM is in continuous counting mode, then the LPTIM will stop at the following match between LPTIM_ARR and LPTIM_CNT registers.</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit can only be set when the LPTIM is enabled. It will be automatically reset by hardware.</p></td></tr><tr height="72.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">ENABLE:</span> LPTIM enable</p><p class="TableIndent">The ENABLE bit is set and cleared by software. </p><p class="TableIndent">0:   LPTIM is disabled</p><p class="TableIndent">1:   LPTIM is enabled</p></td></tr></table></div>


### 33.7.6 LPTIM compare register (LPTIM\_CMP)

Address offset: 0x014

Reset value: 0x0000 0000

Caution:The LPTIM\_CMP register must only be modified when the LPTIM is enabled (ENABLE bit set to ‘1’).

### 33.7.7 LPTIM autoreload register (LPTIM\_ARR)

Address offset: 0x018

Reset value: 0x0000 0001

<div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="73.05px"/><col width="541.12px"/><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 31:16</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="56.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 15:0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">ARR</span><span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:Bold;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">[15:0]:</span> Auto reload value</p><p class="TableIndent">ARR is the autoreload value for the LPTIM.</p><p class="TableIndent">This value must be strictly greater than the CMP[15:0] value.</p></td></tr></table></div>


Caution:The LPTIM\_ARR register must only be modified when the LPTIM is enabled (ENABLE bit set to ‘1’).

### 33.7.8 LPTIM counter register (LPTIM\_CNT)

Address offset: 0x01C

Reset value: 0x0000 0000

<div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="73.05px"/><col width="541.12px"/><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 31:16</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="114.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 15:0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">CNT</span><span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:Bold;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">[15:0]:</span> Counter value</p><p class="TableIndent">When the LPTIM is running with an asynchronous clock, reading the LPTIM_CNT register may return unreliable values. So in this case it is necessary to perform two consecutive read accesses and verify that the two returned values are identical.</p><p class="TableIndent">It should be noted that for a reliable LPTIM_CNT register read access, two consecutive read accesses must be performed and compared. A read access can be considered reliable when the values of the two consecutive read accesses are equal.</p></td></tr></table></div>


### 33.7.9 LPTIM option register (LPTIM\_OR)

Address offset: 0x020

Reset value: 0x0000 0000
